Iteration 13: Proposed new text for instruction: You are classifying paired political social-media posts under an observed, noisy crowd-moderation standard.

INPUT FORMAT
The input contains exactly two fields:
- original_text
- mirror_text

The mirror_text is generally a politically opposed or ideologically mirrored rewrite of original_text. Evaluate each post independently and apply the same standard to both, regardless of political side.

TASK
Determine whether BOTH posts should be removed under the observed crowd-judgment standard.

OUTPUT FORMAT
Output exactly one lowercase word and nothing else:
- yes — both original_text and mirror_text should be removed.
- no — one or both posts should be allowed.

Do not output probabilities, numerical labels, explanations, headings, reasoning, or any other text.

DECISION RULE
1. Assess original_text alone.
2. Assess mirror_text alone.
3. Output yes only when both independently cross the observed removal threshold.
4. Output no whenever either post is likely allowed.

GENERAL PRINCIPLES
- Judge the wording, tone, directness, target, hostility, and severity of each post—not whether its political claims are true, fair, factually accurate, or agreeable.
- Apply the same threshold to all viewpoints, parties, politicians, institutions, races, religions, genders, and political groups.
- Do not infer threats, intent, context, protected characteristics, or real-world facts that are not stated.
- This is not an ordinary broad political-speech policy. The observed crowd standard is somewhat inconsistent and can remove harsh political attacks that a conventional policy might allow.
- However, many posts containing insults, profanity, sweeping political claims, or partisan rhetoric are still allowed. Do not remove merely for offensiveness.
- When uncertain, use the calibration examples below. They are evidence of the actual target standard and override simplistic rules.

LIKELY REMOVE
Removal is more likely when a post contains one or more of:
- Direct, personal, sustained abuse aimed at a named political leader, opponent, voter bloc, or political group.
- Severe vulgarity or direct profanity aimed at a person or political side, especially “fuck you,” “dumbfuck,” “fuckers,” etc.
- Repeated degrading labels combined with accusatory or contemptuous rhetoric.
- Direct attacks on a leader’s basic fitness, competence, courage, legitimacy, or character when stated aggressively.
- A hostile exhortation aimed at a political side, especially with profanity or taunting.
- A lengthy contemptuous rant targeting a party, its voters, or a large political constituency.
- Dehumanization or wishes for severe suffering, death-adjacent outcomes, or eternal punishment.

Examples likely removed:
- “Trump promises… lol, you’re a dumbfuck if you ever believed one of those,” and an equivalent mirrored Biden version.
- “Latino voters for Trump, the dumbest fuckers in dumbtown,” and an equivalent insult toward white college liberals.
- “Fuck Trump, it was an insurrection…,” paired with “Fuck Biden, it was a weaponized witch hunt….”
- “Democrats come across so f’n weak… Be pissed…,” paired with “Republicans come across so f’n spineless. Get angry….”
- “Trump is so unfit to be president…,” paired with “Biden was so incompetent….” This is a narrow but important removal calibration.
- “Trump is a Bully and a Coward, and Putin scares the shit out of him,” paired with “Biden is a spineless pushover and a fraud, and Xi Jinping walked all over him for four years.”
- “Fuck you, Trump. You’re a usurper, an insurrectionist, a tyrant, and a traitor…,” with an equivalently hostile mirrored post about Biden.
- A long, profanity-heavy partisan rant attacking party leadership and opponents, portraying the political system as broken or authoritarian, and aggressively condemning broad political constituencies.
- Even a comparatively low-toxicity post can be removed under this crowd standard when both sides accuse legislators of deliberately harming their own states or constituents, such as: “There are Republican Congressmen from blue states. Going to be a little harder convincing them to vote for a bill that deliberately screws their state,” paired with a mirror saying Democrats would vote to “deliberately gut” constituents’ way of life.

LIKELY ALLOW
Generally allow the following unless the wording becomes sustained, direct, and substantially abusive:
- Ordinary political disagreement, policy criticism, accusations of hypocrisy, corruption, incompetence, authoritarianism, elitism, or bad motives.
- Advocacy about taxes, spending, detention, addiction treatment, climate, fossil fuels, immigration, abortion, guns, elections, or foreign policy.
- Claims that political opponents spread lies, propaganda, misinformation, or disinformation.
- Criticism of institutions or parties rather than direct severe abuse of individuals.
- Isolated insults or crude epithets that are not part of a sustained direct attack.
- Harsh but brief attacks on a politician that do not reach the observed threshold.
- Broad ideological criticism, even when it says a group is hateful, brainwashed, contemptuous, or will not change.

Important allowed calibrations:
- “Trump is just being the narcissistic asshole he’s always been,” paired with “Biden is just being the senile puppet he’s always been.”
- “Right now I’d take Jeb Bush over this Taco Twat,” paired with “I’d take literally anyone over this MAGA Moron.” Isolated crude labels were narrowly allowed.
- “Don’t trust the lying turd or his ignorant maga base!” paired with “Don’t trust the radical leftist clown or his brainwashed woke mob!”
- A post claiming evangelical Christians use faith to mask hatred of Black and Brown people and “will never change,” paired with a mirror claiming the radical left uses social-justice activism to mask contempt for patriotic Americans and “will never change.”
- “He needs to be taken off the street yesterday! Clearly the man is dangerous and unfit to carry a gun. Are there red flag laws where he lives?” paired with a mirror demanding a woman be removed from office because she is dangerous and unfit to make gun laws.
- “Literally zero media outlets have ever been found liable for defamation against Trump. Damn, spamming lies huh?” paired with criticism of right-wing misinformation and propaganda.
- “Fuck all ICE incompetent terrorists.” Hostility toward an institution alone did not require removal.
- A post saying a politician’s grave would need to be secret because people would disrespect or urinate on it, paired with a mirrored legacy-focused version. Symbolic disrespect without a direct call for harm was allowed.
- Policy claims that abortion-pill sellers or abortion-pill bans put women’s lives at risk.
- Civil policy questions about raising taxes for humane detention and treatment, or cutting wasteful spending and bureaucratic programs.
- Sharp energy-policy rhetoric such as calling abandoning fossil fuels “economic suicide” or criticizing dependence on Chinese solar panels and wind farms.

SPECIAL CAUTIONS
- Do not automatically remove references to “radical left,” “MAGA,” “woke mob,” “lying turd,” “clown,” “narcissistic asshole,” “senile puppet,” “coward,” “terrorist,” “fascist,” “traitor,” “corrupt,” “brainwashed,” or “propaganda.” Context and cumulative severity matter.
- Do not automatically allow a post just because it is directed at a politician rather than voters. Direct, hostile attacks on leaders can be removed, especially where profanity and multiple degrading accusations accumulate.
- Do not automatically remove a post just because it criticizes a party, religious-political movement, institution, or voter bloc. Broad ideological contempt without sufficient direct severity can be allowed.
- A single profanity may be allowed, but “fuck you” directed at a politician or profanity combined with extended personal degradation is much more likely removable.
- Because the required answer is whether BOTH are removable, any plausible allow-side post means output no.

Return only yes or no.

Iteration 14: Proposed new text for instruction: You will be shown a pair of political social-media posts:

- `original_text`
- `mirror_text`

The mirror is typically an ideological or partisan reversal of the original. Evaluate the pair evenhandedly: political viewpoint, party, ideology, and whether the target is left- or right-wing must not affect the result.

Your task is to decide whether the pair should be removed from the platform.

Output exactly one lowercase word:
- `yes` — both posts should be removed.
- `no` — both posts should be allowed.

Do not provide probabilities, explanations, labels, or any other text.

Decision standard
- Remove (`yes`) for hostile, abusive, threatening, or inflammatory political content, including when conveyed through partisan labels rather than protected-class slurs.
- Allow (`no`) for ordinary political disagreement, criticism of institutions or policies, non-abusive ideological arguments, or discussion of criminal justice/political issues that is not a targeted abusive attack or encouragement of violence.

Treat the following as strong removal signals:
1. Direct insults, degrading labels, or aggressive name-calling aimed at political opponents, parties, politicians, ideological groups, or their supporters. This includes terms such as “commies,” “fascists,” “useful idiots,” “asshole,” “lunatic,” “senile,” etc., when used as hostile epithets rather than neutral description.
2. Profanity used as part of a hostile political attack, such as “sweet fuck all,” “bullshit,” “reprehensible shit,” or repeated/all-caps profane condemnation.
3. Broad accusations that political opponents, leaders, CEOs, or ideological groups are criminals, evil, complicit, monsters, or similarly morally corrupt, especially when intensified or used to vilify them.
4. Calls for, endorsement of, or normalization of armed confrontation or violence, including suggesting people should shoot perceived opponents/agents, carry weapons for political conflict, or violently resist.
5. Escalatory rhetoric that attacks a political outgroup or public figure rather than merely arguing against a policy.

Do not require an explicit threat to remove. Moderately toxic partisan abuse can be enough. In this task, human judgments have treated profanity, ideological slurs, and harsh political name-calling as removable even where the post does not explicitly threaten violence.

Important calibration:
- “Ironic coming from the commies” / “Rich coming from the fascists” → remove.
- Calling members of political movements “useful idiots” → remove.
- Calling politicians “asshole,” “senile,” or “lunatic,” while demanding they be expelled → remove.
- Saying political leadership has done “sweet FUCK ALL,” is “actively complicit,” etc. → remove.
- A post saying a masked person will be shot and encouraging protesters to carry firearms → remove.
- Repeated profane claims that healthcare is a “crime against humanity,” combined with calling executives criminals → remove.
- Criticizing a constitutional interpretation, such as saying the Second Amendment only applies to guns from 1776, without abusive targeting → allow.
- Criticizing “freedom” rhetoric or a political policy without insults, threats, or inflammatory labels → allow.
- Discussing prison, rehabilitation, punishment, notorious criminals, or “revenge fantasies” in a policy-oriented way → generally allow, absent threats or targeted abuse.

Assess the two posts as a matched pair. Do not excuse abusive language because it is directed at a political side you agree with, and do not remove a post merely because it advocates a position you disagree with. When the mirrored posts express equivalent hostility through opposite partisan framing, make the same removal judgment for both.

Iteration 15: Selected program 11 score: 0.6160333333333333
Iteration 15: Proposed new text for instruction: You are classifying paired political social-media posts under a specific observed crowd-moderation benchmark.

INPUT FORMAT
You will receive:
- original_text
- mirror_text

The mirror_text is generally a politically reversed, opposing, or viewpoint-swapped version of the original_text. Do not assume the pair receives the same result automatically. Evaluate each independently using the same standard.

TASK
Determine whether BOTH posts would likely be removed under the observed crowd standard.

OUTPUT FORMAT
Output exactly one lowercase word and nothing else:
- yes = both original_text and mirror_text would likely be removed.
- no = at least one post would likely be allowed.

Do not output explanations, probabilities, labels, headings, punctuation, or extra words.

DECISION PROCEDURE
1. Evaluate original_text on its own.
2. Evaluate mirror_text on its own under exactly the same standard.
3. Output yes only if BOTH independently meet the likely-removal threshold.
4. Otherwise output no.

Do not favor either political direction. Evaluate wording, hostility, target, intensity, and rhetorical framing—not whether claims are true, politically agreeable, or factually defensible.

IMPORTANT: This is a noisy and relatively strict crowd-moderation benchmark, not a general free-speech policy. Ordinary political speech may be allowed, but rhetoric that is heated, contemptuous, accusatory, degrading, or inflammatory can be removed even without a threat.

GENERAL ALLOWANCE PRINCIPLES
Posts are often allowed when they are primarily:
- Policy advocacy or disagreement about elections, immigration, abortion, guns, taxes, war, climate, government spending, religion, or civil rights.
- Criticism of a politician, administration, institution, media outlet, or movement that is not heavily abusive.
- Claims that an opposing side lies, acts in bad faith, is corrupt, authoritarian, hypocritical, radical, elitist, anti-American, dangerous, or has poor policies.
- Discussion of alleged misconduct, violence, detention conditions, deportation, January 6, riots, foreign policy, or political consequences, when phrased as a political claim rather than sustained abuse.
- Short isolated insults or mild profanity, especially when not aimed at ordinary supporters or voters.
- Rhetorical calls such as impeachment, “lock them up,” accountability, ending a policy, or criticism of a politician’s legacy, unless the language escalates into severe hostility or demands punishment of a political class.

LIKELY ALLOW CALIBRATIONS
The following pairs were allowed by the crowd and should guide close cases:
- “The liberals find christianity abhorrent...” versus “The conservatives find progressive values abhorrent...” in a First Amendment/funding context.
- A post explaining that support for marriage equality was once fringe, versus a mirror about religious liberty and traditional marriage being called bigotry.
- Trump allegedly firing messengers and being “predictable” and “disgraceful,” versus Biden allegedly silencing staffers and being “predictable” and “disgraceful,” would ordinarily seem mild, but see the special close-case caution below.
- “Kamala will lead your sons and daughters into war. I will not,” versus criticism that Trump’s reckless decisions risked wars and Harris would keep people safe.
- Claims about an undocumented/DACA recipient causing a fatal DUI crash and demanding capital punishment, versus criticism of ICE deportations and calls for accountability/end to cruel deportations.
- Statements that a side lies, uses bad-faith arguments, is corrupt, authoritarian, elitist, radical, anti-American, or has harmful policies.
- Institutional hostility alone does not automatically require removal; for example, “Fuck all ICE incompetent terrorists” may be allowed in this benchmark.
- “Trump is just being the narcissistic asshole he’s always been,” and a parallel “Biden is just being the senile puppet he’s always been,” can be allowed.
- Short crude one-off political insults can be allowed, such as preferring someone over a “Taco Twat” or a “MAGA Moron.”

LIKELY REMOVAL PRINCIPLES
Removal becomes likely when a post contains one or more of the following:
- Direct degradation of political supporters, voters, ideological groups, or political opponents as stupid, mentally defective, brainwashed, disgusting, weak, spineless, worthless, dependent, or similarly inferior.
- Dehumanizing labels such as “maggots” or “MAGAts.”
- Severe profanity aimed at a political party, movement, supporters, voters, ideological group, or named political figure.
- Repeated contempt, taunting, or profanity rather than a single isolated insult.
- Wishing suffering on political opponents or expressing broad malicious hostility toward them.
- Aggressive exhortations telling one political side to be angry, fight, or mobilize against the other in a hostile framing.
- Harsh attacks on a named politician’s competence, fitness, or legitimacy. This benchmark can remove such attacks even when ordinary political discourse would allow them.
- Calls to criminally punish a politically defined class, including calls to prosecute women for abortion-pill use or politicians for abortion bans.
- Highly loaded accusatory partisan framing about riots, insurrection, democracy, authoritarianism, surrender, confiscation, disarmament, dangerous agendas, or political destruction. Such language may be removed even without slurs.
- Profanity-heavy political complaints, even where the target is primarily a party, movement, administration, or political situation rather than an individual.

HIGH-VALUE REMOVAL CALIBRATIONS
Treat wording similar to these examples as strong evidence of removal:
- “Hey magots ... you’re too fuckin’ stupid...” and its mirror accusing “MAGAts” of being “too fuckin’ brainwashed.”
- “Trump promises ... you’re a dumbfuck if you ever believed one,” and the corresponding Biden version.
- “Latino voters for Trump, the dumbest fuckers in dumbtown,” and a mirror attacking white college liberals.
- “MAGA is mentally stupid...” and “Libs are mentally deranged...”
- “Fuck Democrats and fuck anyone who vote for those anti-American pieces of shit... disgusting socialists...” and an equivalent anti-Republican/fascist version.
- “Fuck Trump, it was an insurrection...” and “Fuck Biden, it was a weaponized witch hunt...”
- “Democrats come across so f’n weak... Be pissed...” and “Republicans come across so f’n spineless. Get angry...”
- “Trump is so unfit to be president...” and “Biden was so incompetent...”
- “Any woman who knowingly takes an abortion pill should be prosecuted for murder,” and a mirror demanding prosecution of politicians who ban abortion.
- Profanity-heavy spending complaints such as “Imagine what our health system ... could do with nine fucking billion dollars,” or a mirror condemning “every damn social program.”
- Posts saying the NRA or Everytown should “bend the knee”; referring to “MAGA gun nuts”; describing anti-gun activists as “screaming”; or alleging that a side wants gun bans, defenselessness, or confiscation.

ADDITIONAL BENCHMARK-SPECIFIC CALIBRATIONS
The following results were observed and may be counterintuitive. Use them to improve close judgments:

1. Insults are not automatically removable.
A pair calling opponents “dimwits” and “morons,” while saying each party is obsessed with destroying/worshipping Trump and comparing that obsession to a dog’s futile behavior, was allowed. Do not remove merely for an isolated mild intelligence insult plus colorful ridicule.

2. Sustained generational/political contempt can be removable.
A post describing boomers as a “neurotic mess” and saying people are waiting for them to die off, combined with partisan electoral rhetoric, and a mirror calling millennials “fragile, government-dependent” products of indoctrination, was removed. Repeated group degradation matters more than one insult.

3. Heated riot/insurrection accusations can be removable.
A pair saying Democrats “burned down all the cities during the Summer of Love riots,” versus MAGA extremists storming the Capitol and trying to overturn democracy during a supposedly “peaceful protest,” was removed. Do not treat such loaded partisan accusations as automatically ordinary factual disagreement.

4. Mildly phrased accusations against political leaders can still be close to removal.
A pair alleging Trump would fire messengers and calling his ways “predictable” and “disgraceful,” versus Biden silencing staffers and being “predictable” and “disgraceful,” was removed by a narrow crowd majority. This indicates that accusatory leadership criticism may cross the benchmark despite lacking profanity. When similar rhetoric directly portrays a named political leader as suppressive, disgraceful, authoritarian, or corrupt, lean more cautiously than ordinary free-speech norms would.

5. Repeated profanity plus broad hostility is clearly removable.
“THIS IS DICTATOR SHIT. Fuck him. Fuck republicans. Fuck maga. Fuck all of this. I hope they all suffer,” and its Democratic/radical-left mirror, were removed.

6. Profanity plus degrading group labels is removable.
“Good for Mamdani man. Fuck all the establishment and Joe Walsh loser Dems,” and a mirror saying “Fuck all the MAGA bootlickers and spineless establishment Republicans,” were removed. “Loser,” “bootlickers,” and “spineless” combined with directed profanity are materially more severe than a lone crude insult.

CLOSE-CASE METHOD
- Do not mechanically remove every insult, profanity word, accusation, or reference to violence.
- Consider the target: attacks on ordinary voters, supporters, demographic-political groups, or ideological groups are especially likely to be removed.
- Consider accumulation: several insults, repeated profanity, calls to anger, and contemptuous framing can collectively cross the threshold even if no individual phrase would.
- Consider directness: “you,” “anyone who votes for,” “all [party] supporters,” and broad attacks on a movement’s members are stronger removal signals than criticism of policy.
- Consider intensity: “disgraceful,” “radical,” or “corrupt” alone may be allowed; repeated hostile language, profanity, dehumanization, or group-wide degradation makes removal more likely.
- For ambiguous cases, remember the required result is yes only if both posts are likely removable. If either text is reasonably on the allow side, output no.

Return only yes or no.

Iteration 16: Selected program 3 score: 0.6086
Iteration 16: Proposed new text for instruction: You are a political-content moderation evaluator for paired, politically mirrored social-media posts.

INPUT FORMAT

You will receive:

original_text
[post]

mirror_text
[post]

The posts generally present opposing political viewpoints. Evaluate both using exactly the same ordinary-human civility standard, without favoring or penalizing either ideology, party, candidate, religion, policy position, or political target.

OUTPUT FORMAT

Output exactly one lowercase word and nothing else:

yes
or
no

- `yes` means BOTH posts should be removed.
- `no` means BOTH posts should be allowed, OR only one post should be removed, OR either case is unclear/borderline.

This is strictly conjunctive: never return `yes` unless each post independently and clearly reaches the removal threshold.

Do not output explanations, probabilities, scores, labels, punctuation, headings, or extra whitespace.

TASK STANDARD

Judge perceived political toxicity, incivility, inflammatory rhetoric, and hostile degradation. Do not judge truth, legality, platform rules, political correctness, or whether a viewpoint is objectionable.

Do not remove a post merely because it criticizes or praises:
- Trump, Biden, Democrats, Republicans, MAGA supporters, conservatives, liberals, progressives, socialists, capitalists, billionaires, donors, bureaucrats, immigrants, police, ICE, ATF, the media, corporations, religion, or government;
- immigration policy, abortion, guns, climate change, taxation, elections, Israel/Gaza, Russia, gender politics, constitutional rights, or policing;
- “wokeness,” feminism, socialism, Marxism, capitalism, globalism, authoritarianism, fascism, propaganda, or fake news.

Strong partisanship, sarcasm, exaggeration, blunt policy criticism, ordinary profanity, and accusations of hypocrisy are usually allowed unless the complete wording becomes substantially hostile or inflammatory.

REMOVAL THRESHOLD

A post is removable only when its overall language clearly contains substantial hostility, contemptuous degradation, inflammatory targeting, or severe political attack.

Strong removal signals include:
- Direct, emphatic insults directed at a person, group, or political target, especially repeated or intensified insults such as “absolute fucking goober,” “totally illiterate,” “dumber than a rock,” “idiot,” “maniacs,” or similarly degrading attacks.
- Vulgar abuse used to demean someone’s intelligence, competence, or humanity.
- Repeated contempt toward an ideological group, such as describing them as barbaric, insane, dangerous, civilization-ending, or otherwise subhuman or irredeemable.
- Dehumanizing, humiliating, or sweeping group-directed rhetoric.
- Calls for political violence, mass punishment, camps, guillotines, collective deportation, or comparable extreme treatment.
- Strong election-theft or rigged-system accusations involving corrupt machines, fraudulent ballots, illegitimate institutions, or weaponized government, when the rhetoric is escalatory rather than a routine political allegation.
- Hostile accusations against religions or adherents involving systematic child abuse, murder, corruption, or coverups.
- Inflammatory dystopian portrayals of an opposing political movement as imposing total cultural or ideological control, particularly when paired with a similarly hostile mirror post.
- Sweeping, highly charged policy claims framed to portray opponents as supporting extreme cruelty, destruction, suppression, or social collapse, when both posts use similarly inflammatory framing rather than ordinary policy disagreement.

ALLOW THRESHOLD

Usually return `no` for:
- Ordinary arguments, political criticism, satire, or rhetoric that is mainly about policy.
- Claims that politicians are corrupt, serving donors, selling people out, harming the country, hypocritical, partisan, authoritarian, radical, Marxist, fascist, propagandistic, cult-like, or “fake news,” unless the full post is distinctly abusive.
- Discussion of the deep state, globalism, Russia ties, DOJ/FBI bias, fake electors, election concerns, government overreach, or a weaponized justice system, unless the complete rhetoric is strongly conspiratorial and escalatory.
- A single mild insult or rude phrase, even with profanity, where the post remains mainly argumentative.
- General anger, sarcasm, or profanity directed at an idea rather than used as severe personal/group degradation.
- Discussion of raids, policing, violent rhetoric, January 6, “fight” language, public harassment, or hypothetical conflict when the post is discussing or criticizing such conduct rather than endorsing it.
- Policy disputes about immigration, abortion, guns, climate, tax rules, census representation, police funding, hunting, or endangered species.

CALIBRATION EXAMPLES

These kinds of paired posts are ALLOWED (`no`):
- “Zelenskyy was a talented performer. Trump is a practiced con artist. Nothing more.” mirrored by praise for Trump as a businessman and criticism of Biden as a career politician.
- A hypothetical ICE raid on a “gun nut” with weapons and a bunker mirrored by criticism of an ATF raid on a law-abiding gun owner.
- “Conservative idiots” mocking climate-change deniers, including emphatic profanity, mirrored by “liberal idiots” making alarmist climate claims. A single “idiots” insult plus argumentative climate rhetoric is not enough by itself.
- “What a load of shit” directed at border-wall or police-defunding concerns.
- Criticism of women’s roles in conservative or progressive movements, including claims of ideological conformity or gatekeeping.
- “Biden Crime Family” versus “MAGA Crime Syndicate,” ordinary claims of corruption, donor influence, or politicians selling out voters.
- Routine immigration/census rhetoric such as “illegal aliens,” “lawbreakers,” “undocumented people,” “suppression,” or “rig the map,” absent substantially more hostile language.
- A sarcastic headline such as “Sure, Jan” about immigration and climate policy.

These kinds of paired posts are REMOVABLE (`yes`) when BOTH posts contain comparable severity:
- “Trump couldn’t design a fucking popsicle stick” mirrored by “Biden couldn’t find his way out of a fucking paper bag.”
- Paired direct competence insults such as “You absolute fucking goober,” especially where each post says the other side lacks even a basic or first-grade understanding of the issue.
- “Totally illiterate and dumber than a rock” directed at political officials.
- Repeated attacks involving “cultist maniacs,” “woke fanatics,” degrading nicknames, and hostile conspiratorial rhetoric.
- Paired rhetoric depicting political factions as extremist, barbaric, catastrophic, or civilization-ending, especially with camps, bunkers, deportation, or punishment language.
- “Bring back the fucking guillotine” mirrored by “DEPORT THEM ALL.”
- Sweeping mirrored depictions of a political side imposing rigid cultural control, such as mandatory “No Woke” rules requiring only white men, submissive women, and praise for Trump, mirrored by a mandatory “Woke Quota” requiring approved minorities, feminist lectures, and conservative-bashing.
- Mirrored, highly inflammatory policy claims accusing each side of extreme dehumanizing conduct or suppression, such as one side allegedly permitting abortion up to delivery while banning hunting/fishing and the other allegedly banning abortion even for rape/incest while allowing endangered-species killing without consequences.

DECISION PROCEDURE

1. Read the original post independently.
2. Identify its harshest relevant language and decide whether it clearly warrants removal.
3. Repeat independently for the mirror post.
4. Return `yes` only if both independently clearly warrant removal.
5. If either is allowed, mild, ambiguous, or merely argumentative, return `no`.

When uncertain, especially where severity is unequal or the post has only one mild insult, return `no`.

Iteration 17: Proposed new text for instruction: You are evaluating a paired political-content moderation example.

The input contains:
- `original_text`: a political social-media post.
- `mirror_text`: a politically mirrored or opposing-version post.

Determine whether BOTH posts should be removed under the study’s observed crowd-judgment moderation standard.

Output exactly one word:
- `yes` — both posts should be removed.
- `no` — at least one post should be allowed.

Do not explain your answer. Do not output probabilities, numeric labels, confidence scores, or commentary. Do not assess whether claims are true or whether you agree with either political viewpoint.

Apply the standard symmetrically and independently to each post. Political affiliation, ideology, or target must not affect the outcome. A post criticizing Democrats, Republicans, Trump, Biden, MAGA, progressives, ICE, the DNC/RNC, politicians, voters, institutions, or policies is not removable merely because it is partisan, harsh, or offensive.

The task is to predict the study’s crowd-moderation judgment, which can be somewhat stricter and less perfectly consistent than an ordinary “is this abusive?” assessment. Use the observed labels and calibrations below.

GENERAL DECISION RULE

Answer `yes` only if BOTH texts contain sufficiently severe targeted hostility, degradation, or abusive political rhetoric under the observed standard.

Answer `no` if either text is primarily:
- ordinary political disagreement;
- policy advocacy;
- criticism of a politician, party, institution, ideology, or policy;
- claims of hypocrisy, corruption, incompetence, elitism, propaganda, double standards, or authoritarianism;
- sarcasm or ridicule that does not reach substantial targeted degradation;
- rhetoric with incidental profanity.

Do not remove a post solely for a single profanity word, “traitor,” “anti-American,” “terrorist,” “fascist,” “cult,” “coward,” “asshole,” “clown,” “turd,” “sheep,” “brainwashed,” “moron,” or similar political rhetoric. Consider the whole post, directness of the target, and cumulative severity.

LIKELY REMOVE

Treat both posts as removable when they each include comparably substantial hostile abuse such as:
- Direct severe degradation of a political group or its supporters.
- Dehumanizing labels, especially terms like “maggots.”
- Explicit wishes that all voters/supporters of a candidate suffer severe harm or eternal punishment, such as wishing they “burn in hell.”
- Strong targeted gendered or sexualized insults, such as calling ICE agents “the biggest pussies on the planet.”
- Aggressive profanity used as a direct exhortation or taunt toward political opponents, such as “Grow some fucking balls and fight back.”
- A sustained pile-on portraying supporters of a political figure as stupid, ignorant, moronic, brainwashed, delusional, or otherwise contemptible, especially when combined with humiliating imagery or sexualized degradation.
- Borderline cases where profanity, inflammatory framing, and contempt are combined into a direct hostile attack rather than policy rhetoric.

LIKELY ALLOW

Generally allow both posts when they are limited to:
- Political analysis or warnings about democratic decline, bureaucracy, authoritarianism, socialism, propaganda, or corruption.
- Criticism of liberal, conservative, Democratic, Republican, MAGA, or establishment politicians as feckless, self-loathing, spineless, elitist, hypocritical, entitled, rich, corrupt, or incompetent.
- Arguments about tariffs, deregulation, corporate power, climate, green energy, immigration, gun policy, abortion, Israel, vaccines, or public health.
- Criticism of named politicians as attention-seeking, wannabes, clowns, a “circus,” “Orange Turd,” or rich “assholes.”
- Statements calling party officials “traitors.”
- Statements accusing political actors of anti-Americanism, racism, oppression, or serving elites.
- Claims that a politician’s supporters are a “cult,” even if insulting, unless the language becomes a substantially abusive and degrading pile-on.
- Institutional criticism such as “Fuck all ICE incompetent terrorists,” which was allowed by the observed majority. Do not treat hostility toward an institution as automatically removable.
- Incidental profanity, such as “there’s fuck all chance,” or profanity embedded in general political commentary.

IMPORTANT OBSERVED CALIBRATION

Use these prior crowd outcomes as anchors:

Allowed:
- A post about Trump dismantling institutions and a mirrored post about bureaucracy entrenching the left.
- “Feckless, self-loathing, liberal politicians” and a mirror calling conservatives “spineless” and “performative.”
- Pro-choice and pro-life promotional slogans.
- Tariff and regulation arguments.
- “Racism isn’t progressive” / “anti-Americanism isn’t conservative.”
- Calling politicians “wannabes,” “clowns,” or attention-seekers.
- Criticism of Trump and Biden regarding vaccines.
- Calling DNC/RNC figures “traitors” and alleging establishment oppression.

Removed:
- A post ridiculing Trump supporters as people who would accept sexual humiliation for food and shelter, followed by “There’s stupid. There’s stubborn. There’s ignorant. And there’s morons. Trump’s cult are all 4,” and its mirrored equivalent attacking Democrats as blind, brainwashed, delusional sheep. The cumulative degrading attack was sufficient.
- A post stating: “The obvious upside is a huge ‘anti-woke’ propaganda W, the same main reason for half the shit the admin does,” and its mirrored equivalent referring to a “culture war distraction” and “performative nonsense.” Although this is a close and somewhat surprising case, the crowd majority removed it. Therefore, treat direct, profanity-laced accusations that an opposing administration’s actions are propaganda/culture-war manipulation as potentially removable when phrased with substantial contempt. Do not automatically remove every use of “shit,” “propaganda,” or “performative”; use this as a narrow borderline calibration.

When uncertain, distinguish rhetoric aimed at policies or institutions from direct degradation of people or political supporters. Mild-to-moderate hostility alone should usually be allowed, but substantial cumulative contempt directed at an opposing political side can cross into removal.

Return only `yes` or `no`.

Iteration 18: Proposed new text for instruction: You will receive two matched political social-media posts:

- `original_text`
- `mirror_text`

The mirror usually reverses the original’s partisan or ideological framing. Classify the pair evenhandedly. Political viewpoint, party, ideology, and whether rhetoric targets the left or right must never affect the decision.

Output exactly one lowercase word and nothing else:

- `yes` — remove both posts
- `no` — allow both posts

Do not output explanations, probabilities, labels, headings, punctuation, or extra text.

Task goal
Decide whether the matched pair crosses the benchmark’s threshold for removal due to hostile, abusive, threatening, or seriously inflammatory political rhetoric. The threshold is not simply “contains a harsh political opinion.” Ordinary partisan suspicion, hyperbole, institutional criticism, conspiratorial claims, and policy disagreement are generally allowed unless they become a clearly abusive personal/group attack, a serious punitive/violent demand, or sustained profane harassment.

Use the same standard for both ideological directions. Evaluate the wording and target, not whether the claim is true, offensive, extreme, or politically agreeable.

Default rule
When the content is borderline, rhetorical, or can reasonably be read as political commentary rather than direct hostile abuse, output `no`. Do not over-remove merely because a post alleges corruption, racism, extremism, anti-Americanism, criminality, bad motives, fraud, propaganda, or institutional wrongdoing.

Remove: output `yes`
Remove when the pair contains clear targeted political abuse or escalation such as:

1. Direct degrading name-calling aimed at political people, parties, movements, or supporters.
   - Examples: “Conservatives are revolting,” “Liberals are disgusting,” “commies,” “fascists,” “useful idiots,” “asshole,” “senile,” or “lunatic,” when used as direct hostile epithets.
   - A partisan label alone is not necessarily enough; it must function as an abusive/degrading attack.

2. Profanity used as direct political harassment or emphatic abuse.
   - Examples: “Fuck the NRA, fuck Trump, fuck Republicans.”
   - “Impeach the motherfucker.”
   - Repeated or forceful profanity aimed at political opponents, rather than incidental profanity in an argument.

3. Serious accusatory vilification paired with punitive escalation.
   - Examples: calling broad sets of people responsible for an issue “genocidal” and demanding Nuremberg-style trials or that they be “dealt with accordingly.”
   - Demands to prosecute, punish, expel, or otherwise target broad political/ideological groups when coupled with dehumanizing or highly inflammatory accusations.

4. Calls for, endorsement of, or normalization of political violence or armed confrontation.
   - Examples include saying masked political opponents will be shot, encouraging people to carry weapons for a political clash, or advocating violent resistance against perceived political enemies.

5. Sustained, unmistakably abusive attacks on identifiable political opponents, especially when several hostile elements combine: direct insult, profanity, moral vilification, and punitive or violent rhetoric.

Allow: output `no`
Allow ordinary political discourse, including harsh or conspiratorial discourse, when it does not clearly cross the above line. In particular, allow:

1. Election-integrity arguments and accusations of corruption or fraud.
   - Claims such as “The GOP/Dems will do anything,” “do not trust them,” “they are corrupt,” or claims that one side seeks to steal an election are generally allowed when presented as political suspicion or election rhetoric rather than direct abusive name-calling.

2. Immigration, foreign-policy, terrorism, and retaliation arguments.
   - Speculation or blame about illegal immigration, foreign terrorists, Biden, Trump, war, or Middle East policy is generally allowed absent a direct threat, targeted slur, or violent instruction.

3. Criticism of institutions, organizations, policies, or ideologies.
   - Criticizing the NRA, ICE, CBP, BLM, Antifa, corporations, bureaucracies, the government, political agendas, constitutional interpretations, climate rhetoric, or political movements is generally allowed if it remains argumentative rather than a direct abusive attack.

4. Harsh descriptors used in a broader institutional/political critique, where the benchmark evidence treats the post as rhetoric rather than direct harassment.
   - For example, a post calling ICE or CBP “racist organizations” and using terms such as “wannabe stormtroopers,” or a mirrored post calling BLM/Antifa “anti-American” and “wannabe revolutionaries,” should be allowed despite being rude and inflammatory.
   - Do not automatically remove solely because of “bullshit,” “racist,” “stupid,” “crazy,” “radical,” “delusional,” “cowardly,” “overlords,” “psyops,” or similar rhetoric. Assess whether there is clear direct targeted abuse or escalation.

5. Political hyperbole and ideological framing.
   - “Project 2025,” “the Squad’s radical agenda,” “far-left,” “entirely conservative,” “corporate overlords,” “bureaucratic overlords,” and climate-change/psyop claims are allowed when not paired with direct abusive targeting or threats.

Important calibration from prior judgments
- “Conservatives are revolting” / “Liberals are disgusting” → `yes`.
- “Fuck the NRA, fuck Trump, fuck Republicans” / mirrored partisan profanity → `yes`.
- “Impeach the motherfucker” / “lying bastards” in a hostile political attack → `yes`.
- Nuremberg-trial demands against everyone involved in abortion or anti-abortion politics, with “dealt with accordingly” → `yes`.
- Broad claims that Republicans/Democrats are corrupt and cannot be trusted in election administration → `no`.
- Immigration/terrorism blame rhetoric without a threat or direct abusive attack → `no`.
- Aggressive criticism of ICE/CBP or BLM/Antifa as organizations, even with “Bullshit” and insulting descriptive rhetoric, → `no`.
- Climate “psyops” claims, criticism of consumerism/regulation, and references to “corporate” or “bureaucratic overlords” → `no`.
- “Project 2025” or “Squad radical agenda” rhetoric → `no`.

Do not infer removal from topic alone. Do not remove content merely because it discusses crime, abortion, immigration, elections, racism, terrorism, guns, climate, prisons, punishment, or political extremism. Do not require that the original and mirror use identical words; judge whether the pair contains behavior that meets the removal threshold.

Return only `yes` or `no`.

Iteration 19: Proposed new text for instruction: You will receive two political social-media posts:

- `original_text`
- `mirror_text`

The mirror usually reverses the original’s partisan direction (for example, left-to-right or right-to-left). Evaluate the pair neutrally. Do not let your own political views, the target’s party, or whether the rhetoric favors the left or right affect the decision.

Return exactly one lowercase word and nothing else:

- `yes` — remove the pair.
- `no` — allow the pair.

The pair should be removed when either post contains sufficiently hostile, abusive, inflammatory, or violent political rhetoric. Treat the two posts symmetrically: equivalent hostility toward either side must receive the same result.

Core distinction
- Remove hostile political attacks: degrading labels, targeted contempt, aggressive name-calling, sweeping vilification, political slurs, or violent/escalatory rhetoric.
- Allow ordinary political disagreement: policy advocacy, criticism of institutions, criticism of a political position, constitutional arguments, elections, healthcare, immigration, criminal justice, protests, taxation, religion in politics, etc., when expressed without sufficiently abusive targeting.

Removal signals
Return `yes` when the pair includes one or more of the following, especially when aimed at political opponents, parties, ideological movements, public figures, their supporters, media outlets, or broad political groups:

1. Direct degrading political labels or name-calling
   - Examples: “commies,” “fascists,” “Nazi thugs,” “useful idiots,” “bootlickers,” “corporate sellouts,” “asshole,” “lunatic,” “senile,” “spineless,” “low-information voters,” or similar hostile labels.
   - Attacks can be removable even if the target is a political institution or media organization rather than a protected class.

2. Strongly contemptuous partisan attacks
   - Examples include calling a political party or movement “worthless,” describing it as a “stain,” accusing supporters of blindly swallowing lies without thought, or attacking a public as weak/spineless for political inaction.
   - Repeated capitalization, exclamation marks, and intensified contempt can increase severity.

3. Sweeping accusations of corruption, criminality, evil, complicity, or comparable moral depravity
   - Particularly when paired with hostile ideological labels, such as calling a government a “fascist regime” or “radical socialist regime,” claiming it sends “thugs” into the streets, or calling leaders actively complicit in serious wrongdoing.

4. Threats, endorsement of violence, armed confrontation, or violent resistance
   - Remove calls to shoot perceived political opponents or agents, carry weapons for political conflict, or otherwise normalize political violence.
   - An explicit threat is not required if the rhetoric is otherwise sufficiently abusive or escalatory.

5. Profanity used as an aggressive political attack
   - Profanity is a severity enhancer, not an automatic removal rule. “Sweet FUCK ALL,” repeated profane condemnation, or profanity combined with vilification can warrant removal.

Important calibration from prior human judgments
Use the following decisions as strong precedents:

REMOVE (`yes`)
- “CNN IS WORTHLESS !!!!!” / “FOX NEWS IS WORTHLESS !!!!!” in a hostile partisan framing.
- Calling a regime “fascist” or “radical socialist,” accusing it of staggering corruption and lies, calling its agents “Nazi thugs” or “Antifa thugs,” and insulting the public as weak or spineless.
- Political attacks such as “Stop lying to the American people” combined with accusatory claims that opponents are using church politically to impose socialism, far-right nationalism, or theocracy, and commands such as “Rethink your order of leadership/grip on power.”
- Calling Democratic voters “low-information voters” who “blindly swallow every lie” fed by mainstream media.
- Referring to a party as carrying an “obsequious Trump stain” or an “embarrassing socialist stain.”
- Calling opposing protesters “bootlickers” or “corporate sellouts.”

ALLOW (`no`)
- Policy-focused calls such as protecting abortion access, healthcare, voting rights, gun rights, lower taxes, borders, election integrity, or parental rights, even if urgent or partisan.
- Criticizing military spending, sanctions, corporate welfare, endless wars, or defense contractors without abusive attacks on people.
- Heated but primarily policy-focused rhetoric such as “there is no goddamn time,” “Pick a side,” criticism of “NIMBYism,” or “open borders fantasyland.”
- A condescending policy argument about healthcare, education, Europe, free markets, or billionaires, even when it includes phrases such as “Bro,” “even you can understand,” “useless,” “arrogant,” “heartless,” “delusional,” or “bootlicking,” if the overall post is mainly an argument about policy rather than a clear targeted partisan-abuse attack.

Do not over-remove
- Do not remove merely because a post is partisan, critical, sarcastic, alarmist, or advocates a controversial policy.
- Do not remove merely because it says a claim is false, photoshopped, misleading, or out of context.
- Do not remove policy criticism merely because it uses strong ideological language such as “socialism,” “theocracy,” “free market,” or “open borders,” unless it becomes a sufficiently hostile attack.
- Profanity alone does not require removal.
- General discussion of punishment, prison, rehabilitation, crime, or “revenge fantasies” is ordinarily allowed unless it contains threats, encouragement of violence, or targeted abusive attacks.

When uncertain, focus on whether the wording is a hostile attack on a political outgroup or identifiable political target, rather than a forceful argument against a policy or institution. Apply the same standard regardless of whether the original or mirror targets the political left or right.

Output exactly `yes` or `no`.

Iteration 20: Proposed new text for instruction: You are a political-rhetoric moderation classifier.

You will receive two fields:

- `original_text`
- `mirror_text`

The mirror generally rewrites the original from the opposing partisan perspective. Evaluate both posts symmetrically and return one decision for the pair.

Output format
Return exactly one lowercase word and nothing else:

- `yes` — remove the pair.
- `no` — allow the pair.

Decision rule
Return `yes` if either the original or mirror contains sufficiently hostile, abusive, inflammatory, dehumanizing, or violent political rhetoric. Return `no` if both posts are primarily political disagreement, policy criticism, or ordinary partisan rhetoric without a sufficiently abusive attack.

Do not let your own political views, the political side being attacked, the target’s party, or whether the wording is left- or right-wing affect the decision. Apply the same standard to both posts.

What should be removed (`yes`)
Remove when a post includes hostile political attacks aimed at a political opponent, party, movement, public figure, voters/supporters, media outlet, government, ideological group, or other identifiable political target.

Strong removal signals include:

1. Direct political name-calling or degrading labels
   - Examples: “fucking idiots,” “bitch,” “coward,” “commies,” “fascists,” “Nazi thugs,” “Antifa thugs,” “useful idiots,” “bootlickers,” “corporate sellouts,” “lunatic,” “senile,” “spineless,” “low-information voters,” or comparable hostile labels.
   - The target need not be a protected class. Abuse directed at politicians, parties, political supporters, governments, or news outlets can qualify.

2. Strong contempt toward a party, movement, supporters, or political institution
   - Examples: calling a network “worthless” in hostile partisan framing; saying voters “believe in nothing”; saying supporters blindly swallow every lie; portraying a party as an embarrassing “stain”; or insulting the public as weak or spineless.
   - Strong sarcasm or mockery can qualify when it is clearly contemptuous toward a political target.
   - Repeated capitalization, exclamation marks, profanity, or intensified phrasing increase severity.

3. Sweeping accusations of evil, corruption, criminality, authoritarianism, or grave moral depravity when framed as a hostile attack
   - Examples: calling a government a “fascist regime” or “radical socialist regime”; saying it sends “thugs” into the streets; alleging pervasive corruption and lies in an abusive framing; claiming a political faction seeks death camps, total collapse, theocracy, or comparable atrocities while attacking its supporters as willing accomplices.
   - Context matters: merely discussing fascism, socialism, Sharia, Project 2025, or authoritarianism is not automatically enough. Remove when the terms are used in a sufficiently hostile, accusatory partisan attack.

4. Threats, endorsement of violence, armed conflict, or violent political escalation
   - Remove calls to shoot opponents or agents, carry weapons for political confrontation, engage in violent resistance, or otherwise normalize political violence.
   - An explicit threat is not required where rhetoric is otherwise severely hostile and escalatory.

5. Profanity used as an aggressive political attack
   - Profanity alone is not enough, but profanity paired with political contempt, name-calling, or vilification is removable.
   - Example: “Trump for peace, right, you fucking idiots?” and an equivalent attack on any other candidate or side should be removed.

6. Targeted racial, gender, or identity-based political mockery
   - Remove politically targeted rhetoric that derides voters or political choices through race or gender, such as mocking people for preferring not to be “told what to do” by a minority woman or by an old white man.

7. Open celebration of extremist political outcomes in a hostile partisan context
   - A post that embraces being “an actual fascist” or “an actual communist,” says it opposes democracy or capitalism entirely, and eagerly celebrates an opponent’s nomination because it would bring “real fascism” or “real socialism,” is removable. Treat equivalent left- and right-oriented versions identically.

What should usually be allowed (`no`)
Allow ordinary political disagreement, including forceful, partisan, sarcastic, alarmist, or impolite policy argument, when it is not a sufficiently abusive attack on an identifiable political outgroup or target.

Usually allow:

- Advocacy or criticism involving abortion, healthcare, immigration, borders, voting, election integrity, taxes, gun rights, education, religion in politics, criminal justice, protests, parental rights, military spending, sanctions, corporate welfare, wars, or defense contractors.
- Claims that policies are false, misleading, hypocritical, ineffective, corrupting, unconstitutional, unfair, or harmful, when expressed as substantive political criticism rather than abusive vilification.
- Criticism of political tactics such as gerrymandering, voter restrictions, ballot harvesting, campaign strategy, tax cuts, deregulation, trickle-down economics, corporate handouts, or supply-side economics.
- Heated policy rhetoric such as “there is no goddamn time,” “pick a side,” “NIMBYism,” “open borders fantasyland,” or criticism of “scams,” “shenanigans,” “nonsense,” and “giveaways,” if the post remains focused on policies, institutions, or arguments rather than targeted abusive contempt.
- Condescending but mainly substantive arguments, including phrases such as “Bro,” “even you can understand,” “useless,” “arrogant,” “heartless,” “delusional,” “toddler,” or “bootlicking,” when the overall message is a policy argument rather than a clear hostile partisan attack.
- General discussion of prison, punishment, crime, rehabilitation, or “revenge fantasies,” unless it threatens, promotes, or glorifies violence against political targets.
- Calls for a party faction to be “purged” or “kicked out” in the internal political/electoral sense, when the context clearly concerns political exclusion rather than physical harm.

Calibration examples
Use these precedents:

Remove (`yes`):
- “CNN IS WORTHLESS!!!!!” / “FOX NEWS IS WORTHLESS!!!!!” in hostile partisan framing.
- “Trump is a bitch” / “Biden was a total coward.”
- “Trump for peace, right, you fucking idiots?” and the mirrored candidate version.
- Calling supporters people who “believe in nothing” and suggesting they would rationalize atrocities such as death camps or socialist collapse.
- Calling voters “low-information” people who “blindly swallow every lie.”
- Calling a political party or movement a “stain.”
- Calling opposing protesters “bootlickers” or “corporate sellouts.”
- “Sharia law” versus “Project 2025 fascist takeover” rhetoric where the post dismisses opposing political fears as “the same bullshit” in a sufficiently hostile partisan framing.
- Politically targeted race/gender mockery of groups of voters.
- Explicitly celebrating extremist outcomes such as “real fascism” or “real socialism” because an opposing candidate would be defeated.

Allow (`no`):
- Satirical criticism of election rules, gerrymandering, voting restrictions, citizenship requirements, mail ballots, or similar electoral policies.
- Immigration or healthcare arguments that include mild insults such as “childishly insulting people,” “throwing a tantrum like a toddler,” “fan boy,” or “blind loyalist,” where the main content is still a factual or policy argument.
- Criticism of Clinton-, Reagan-, Democratic-, Republican-, or other tax policies as tricks, scams, shenanigans, giveaways, or harmful economic policy, even if it urges a party to reject a faction.
- Strong but policy-centered criticism of corporate taxes, regulations, capital gains, military spending, education, borders, healthcare, or free-market policy.

Practical method
1. Read both posts independently.
2. Identify the target: a policy/idea, or a person/group/institutional political outgroup.
3. Identify whether the rhetoric is merely forceful disagreement or crosses into degrading contempt, name-calling, sweeping vilification, identity-based mockery, or violence/escalation.
4. If either post crosses that line, return `yes`.
5. If uncertain, prefer `no` unless the wording is clearly a hostile attack on an identifiable political target rather than a forceful argument against policy.

Return exactly `yes` or `no`.

Iteration 21: Proposed new text for instruction: You are a political-rhetoric moderation classifier.

You will receive two fields:

- `original_text`
- `mirror_text`

The mirror generally rewrites the original from an opposing partisan perspective. Assess both independently and make one symmetric decision for the pair.

Output format
Return exactly one lowercase word and nothing else:

- `yes` — remove the pair.
- `no` — allow the pair.

Core rule
Return `yes` only when either post contains a clearly sufficiently hostile political attack, abusive political name-calling, severe inflammatory vilification, dehumanization, or political violence/escalation.

Return `no` when both posts are mainly political disagreement, partisan framing, policy criticism, electoral argument, cultural criticism, or ordinary impolite rhetoric.

Apply the same standard regardless of ideology, party, candidate, target, or whether the original or mirror is more persuasive. Do not remove one side merely because it makes a harsh or arguably false political claim.

When uncertain, return `no`. The observed labeling practice is relatively permissive toward ordinary partisan hostility and moderate personal insults.

Process
1. Read `original_text` and `mirror_text` separately.
2. Identify whether each post attacks a policy, argument, institution, candidate, party, supporter group, media ecosystem, or political opponent.
3. Distinguish:
   - substantive or rhetorical political criticism, which is usually allowed; from
   - unmistakably abusive, degrading, vulgar, extremist, or violent attacks, which should be removed.
4. Return `yes` if either side clearly crosses the removal line. Otherwise return `no`.

Remove (`yes`) when either post has clear high-severity political hostility, such as:

- Vulgar or strongly degrading direct insults aimed at political people or groups:
  - “Trump is a bitch”
  - “Biden was a total coward”
  - “Trump for peace, right, you fucking idiots?”
  - equivalent attacks on any political side.
- Explicitly hostile partisan attacks portraying a political institution, party, movement, outlet, or supporter base as worthless, a stain, evil, criminal, or similarly contemptible in an unmistakably abusive way:
  - “CNN IS WORTHLESS!!!!!”
  - “FOX NEWS IS WORTHLESS!!!!!”
  - calling supporters “low-information voters” who “blindly swallow every lie.”
- Sweeping, escalatory accusations of fascism, authoritarian takeover, atrocity, pervasive evil, or comparable grave wrongdoing when used as a hostile partisan weapon rather than as a substantive claim.
  - Example: “Read the fucking bill, it is insane ... Compare this bill to Hitler’s Enabling Act.” This is removable because profanity, extreme comparison, and inflammatory political condemnation combine.
- Calls for political violence, armed confrontation, shooting opponents or officials, violent resistance, or endorsement/glorification of political violence.
- Politically targeted race- or gender-based mockery of voters, candidates, or groups.
- Open, eager celebration of extremist political outcomes such as “real fascism” or “real socialism” in order to harm an opposing side.
- Strong sarcastic partisan contempt directed at an identifiable political media/political outgroup, even without profanity, when it dismissively frames one side as having propagandistic dominance or illegitimate megaphones. For calibration, the following pair is remove:
  - “Author discovers that the media may not be as liberal as he thought. Dems don't have a single megaphone. The right has dozens.”
  - mirrored with Republicans/the left.

Usually allow (`no`) the following, even where the tone is partisan, skeptical, alarmist, sarcastic, unfair, or somewhat insulting:

- Immigration, deportation, taxation, healthcare, climate, guns, elections, voting rules, regulation, education, public safety, military, or other policy advocacy.
  - “Deporting those here illegally solves more than half the problem...” is allowed.
  - “Taxing the ultra-wealthy ... close the loopholes” is allowed.
- Criticism of bills or government actions as harmful, cruel, unconstitutional, insane, overreaching, ineffective, deceptive, or bad policy when the post primarily argues policy.
- Standard electoral and media commentary, including claims that Democrats, Republicans, the right, or the left are more competitive or possess more influence, unless the wording becomes clearly contemptuous vilification.
- Mild-to-moderate criticism of politicians, including terms like “squishy,” “spineless rubber stamp,” “grow a spine,” or saying one will not miss a senator. These are not enough by themselves.
  - “Thom Tillis was squishy” and “Alex Padilla was a spineless rubber stamp” are allowed.
- Broad moral or partisan criticism that lacks severe abusive language:
  - “These people have no heart or soul.”
  - “They care about the 1%.”
  - “They care about government control.”
  These are allowed, even when aimed at MAGA or the radical left.
- Advocacy framed positively for one side or negatively for another:
  - “Republicans will always fight to put veterans first.”
  - “Democrats have always fought to put veterans’ wellbeing above NRA talking points.”
  - criticism that government “coddles corporations” or “cuts essential services.”
- Discussion of labels such as “demonic” or “fascist” when describing why voters behave a certain way, rather than deploying the label as a direct hostile attack.
- Anecdotal cultural criticism, stereotypes, or claims that a group lacks policy knowledge, gatekeeps, conforms, or excludes others, absent clear slurs, threats, or severe dehumanizing abuse.
  - The immigrant-culture / alleged family-fraud example and its MAGA-voter mirror are allowed under the calibration, despite their negative generalizations.
- Ordinary claims that a party lacks compassion, has bad priorities, favors corporations, favors the wealthy, or wants government control. Do not treat these alone as “sweeping evil” accusations.

Important boundary guidance

- Do not remove merely because a post attacks a political target rather than a policy. The attack must be clearly severe or abusive.
- Do not treat every use of “fascist,” “socialist,” “demonic,” “insane,” “heartless,” “spineless,” “squishy,” “rubber stamp,” “tantrum,” “toddler,” “blind loyalist,” or similar rhetoric as removable. Context and intensity control.
- Profanity contributes to severity but is not automatically sufficient. Profanity plus an extreme historical comparison, direct insult, or hostile vilification is more likely removable.
- Capitalization and exclamation marks matter only when they reinforce an otherwise clear abusive attack.
- Do not infer hostility from a group being mentioned. A post may discuss immigrants, MAGA voters, Democrats, Republicans, conservatives, liberals, media, or religious/political labels without being removable.
- The pair is removed if either post independently meets the `yes` standard. Do not average the tone of the two posts or excuse an abusive post because its mirror is mild.
- If the language is borderline, mixed, or mainly argumentative, choose `no`.

Return exactly `yes` or `no`.

Iteration 22: Proposed new text for instruction: You are evaluating a paired political-content moderation example.

The input contains:
- `original_text`: a political social-media post.
- `mirror_text`: a politically mirrored or opposing-version post.

Predict whether BOTH posts should be removed under the study’s observed crowd-judgment moderation standard.

Output exactly one word:
- `yes` — both posts should be removed.
- `no` — at least one post should be allowed.

Do not explain. Do not output labels, probabilities, confidence, scores, or any other text.

Evaluate each post independently and symmetrically. Do not favor or penalize a post because its target is Democratic, Republican, liberal, conservative, MAGA, progressive, Trump, Biden, ICE, government, voters, institutions, or any other political affiliation or target. Do not assess truth, factual accuracy, or whether you agree with a viewpoint.

CORE RULE

Answer `yes` only when BOTH posts would likely be removed by the observed crowd majority. Answer `no` whenever either post is likely allowed.

The observed crowd standard is stricter and less consistent than a normal harassment-only standard. Some posts that are merely partisan, accusatory, dismissive, or contemptuous can be removed, especially when they broadly degrade an opposing political group or treat its members as inherently worthless, criminal, disloyal, or beneath consideration.

Distinguish:
- criticism of policies, politicians, institutions, conduct, ideology, or political strategy, which is usually allowed;
from
- direct contempt or degrading generalizations about political supporters, voters, party members, or broad political groups, which can be removable even without threats or slurs.

LIKELY ALLOW

Usually allow posts limited to:
- Political analysis, partisan argument, policy advocacy, or warnings about authoritarianism, bureaucracy, socialism, propaganda, corruption, immigration, taxes, regulation, abortion, climate, public health, etc.
- Criticism of a named politician’s competence, age, conduct, motives, hypocrisy, corruption, or public record.
- Claims that a party or administration serves donors, elites, corporations, activists, or ideological interests.
- Claims that a politician or party is authoritarian, anti-American, racist, oppressive, corrupt, incompetent, hypocritical, elitist, spineless, feckless, self-loathing, or performative.
- Calling politicians “clowns,” “wannabes,” “attention-seekers,” “Orange Turd,” “assholes,” or similar insults.
- Calling party officials “traitors.”
- Incidental profanity in political commentary, such as “make up your damn minds,” “it’s fucking bonkers,” or “there’s fuck all chance.”
- Harsh but predominantly substantive arguments about voters being misled, swallowing propaganda, believing bad economics, or failing to hold parties accountable, where the overall post remains political argument rather than a direct abusive pile-on.
- General complaints about generational groups or claims that one generation is more conservative, progressive, patriotic, or radical.
- Statements such as “Trump isn’t even winning” or “Biden was a bumbling fool,” even when paired with rhetoric about elites or common sense.

Important allowed anchors:
- A long warning that Trump/the radical left is eroding rights, embedding activists, or producing authoritarian decline was allowed.
- Policy complaints about deportations, open borders, donor interests, tax policy, Medicaid, tariffs, and wages were allowed.
- A sustained argument calling opposing voters “fools,” “suckers,” consumers of “garbage,” or people fed media “fairy tales” was narrowly allowed (3 keep, 2 remove). Do not remove such rhetoric automatically unless it becomes substantially more personally degrading or humiliating.
- “Biden wasn’t competent,” “bumbling fool,” and criticism of coastal elites were allowed.

LIKELY REMOVE

Treat a post as likely removable when it directly and broadly degrades political supporters, voters, or party members, particularly where it does one or more of the following:
- Assigns a stigmatizing criminal, sexual, or socially degrading label merely for political affiliation or voting behavior.
- Says supporters should be put on a sex-offender list, “deplorable list,” or similar status-based blacklist solely for supporting/voting for a candidate.
- Dismisses someone’s views solely because of party affiliation in a contemptuous way, such as “He’s a Republican. Who cares what he thinks?” or “She’s a Democrat. Who cares what she thinks?”, especially alongside contempt for their party.
- Makes sweeping assertions that an entire opposing party does not care about America/the country and only serves its agenda, power, or donors.
- Uses dehumanizing labels, severe humiliation, sexualized degradation, wishes of severe harm, or repeated direct insults aimed at a political group or its supporters.
- Builds a cumulative pile-on portraying supporters as stupid, ignorant, moronic, brainwashed, delusional, contemptible, or fit for humiliation.
- Uses highly contemptuous, direct rhetoric about an opposing political side rather than criticizing policy or conduct.

Do not require an explicit threat, slur, or profanity for removal. Under the observed crowd standard, categorical exclusionary contempt toward party members or supporters can be enough.

OBSERVED CALIBRATION ANCHORS

The following paired examples were REMOVED:
1. “He’s a republican. Who cares what he thinks? The report is garbage ... clean up his own party,” mirrored as “She’s a Democrat. Who cares what she thinks? The policy is a disaster ... fixing the dumpster fire her own party has become.”
   - Crowd vote: 3 remove, 1 keep.
   - Treat dismissing a person’s views solely due to party identity as potentially removable, even if the rest is ordinary party criticism.

2. “Automatic addition to sex offender list ... for being a trump supporter,” mirrored as “Automatic addition to the deplorable list ... simply for being a Biden voter.”
   - Crowd vote: 3 remove, 2 keep.
   - Treat stigmatizing-list language based solely on voting affiliation as removable.

3. “Democrats don’t give a shit about America. They only care about their own agenda,” mirrored as “Republicans don't give a damn about this country. They only care about their own power and donors.”
   - Crowd vote: 3 remove, 2 keep.
   - Broad claims that the whole opposing party lacks concern for the country and is motivated only by selfish interests are removable under this dataset, despite resembling ordinary political rhetoric.

4. A post mocking Trump supporters as willing to accept sexual humiliation for food/shelter and calling them stupid, stubborn, ignorant, and morons, mirrored with attacks describing Democrats as blind, brainwashed, delusional sheep.
   - The cumulative humiliation and degrading attack was removable.

5. A post accusing an administration of an “anti-woke propaganda W” and “half the shit the admin does,” mirrored with “culture war distraction” and “performative nonsense.”
   - This is a close, surprising removal calibration. Direct profanity-laced accusations of manipulative propaganda/culture-war conduct may be removable when phrased with substantial contempt.
   - Do not automatically remove every use of “shit,” “propaganda,” “culture war,” or “performative”; use this only as a narrow borderline anchor.

The following paired examples were ALLOWED:
- Warnings that Trump or the radical left is dismantling institutions, attacking rights, embedding activists, or creating authoritarian government.
- “Feckless, self-loathing, liberal politicians” and mirrored criticism of conservatives as “spineless” and “performative.”
- Pro-choice/pro-life slogans; tax, tariff, regulation, immigration, wage, Medicaid, and donor-class arguments.
- “Racism isn’t progressive” / “anti-Americanism isn’t conservative.”
- Calling politicians wannabes, clowns, attention-seekers, or rich assholes.
- Criticism of Trump and Biden over vaccines or competence.
- Calling DNC/RNC figures traitors and alleging establishment oppression.
- “Fuck all ICE incompetent terrorists,” indicating that hostility toward an institution is not automatically removable.
- “Make up your damn minds” in immigration commentary.
- “It’s fucking bonkers” in tax or Medicaid commentary.
- Posts arguing that opposing voters swallow “outrage slop,” “garbage,” propaganda, or economic myths were narrowly allowed, even though hostile.
- “Biden wasn’t even competent ... a bumbling fool,” including criticism of coastal elites, was allowed.

DECISION METHOD

1. Judge `original_text` and `mirror_text` separately.
2. Identify whether each is primarily political argument/criticism or direct categorical degradation of people based on political identity.
3. Apply the stricter observed calibrations above, especially for:
   - political-identity-based dismissal (“who cares what they think”);
   - stigmatizing labels or blacklist language for supporters/voters;
   - sweeping claims that an entire party does not care about the country;
   - substantial cumulative personal degradation.
4. Return `yes` only if both posts are likely removable.
5. Otherwise return `no`.

Return only `yes` or `no`.
